Environmental, Social and Governance Report

After the tender is completed, the Group strictly implements site management during the construction
process, and the Company, the project department technician, safety officer, material officer and other
management personnel together perform inspection and acceptance on the civilised construction, material
acceptance, storage and other aspects of construction activities in accordance with the industry norms and
environmental protection requirements. During the procurement and design process, we give priority to the
use of energy-saving and environmentally friendly materials and technologies if they are of same quality, and
phase out construction processes with outdated capacity and high environmental pollution.

An annual supplier evaluation is carried out every year, by assigning comprehensive scores to suppliers based
on their quantity, quality, energy saving, timeliness of supply, price reasonableness, bidding price, contract
performance, ability to co-operate, payment benchmark and etc., and grading suppliers according to their
scores to continuously improve supply chain management. In terms of management, we actively promote
new technologies and new materials encouraged by the state, and form an inspection team to inspect the
environmental aspects of the supplier’s production factory before the supplier is admitted.

In terms of environmental protection, energy saving and emission reduction, preference is given to products
that yield more environmentally friendly results. Focus factors of consideration include:

1.   Product performance: Compare the environmental protection and energy saving performance indicators
     of products, such as energy consumption and emission standards, and give priority to the use of
     products that have obtained environmental approvals, such as energy labelling and green certification.

2.   Brands and suppliers: Give priority to the use of reputable suppliers that focus on environmental
     protection, and ensure that they implement environmental protection measures in the manufacturing,
     packaging, transportation and other processes, and provide professional technical support.

3.   Material selection: Be mindful of the composition and source of product materials, and give priority to
     the use of environmentally friendly materials, renewable resources and non-toxic and harmless products,
     to avoid potential harm to the environment and human health.

The Group will consistently improve and strengthen supplier management by taking following measures:

1.   Conducting comprehensive evaluation and contract management: Strengthening the comprehensive
     assessment of suppliers and enhancing the management and supervision of contracts to ensure that
     the contract terms are clearly stated, and the rights and responsibilities are clearly defined. Focus on risk
     management, and set up a reasonable default liability and penalty mechanism to effectively protect the
     interests of the Company.

2.   Enhancing transparency and informatization level: Strengthening the visibility and transparency of the
     supply chain, and establishing a supplier information sharing platform, to timely grasp the supplier’s
     production progress, inventory and delivery capacity. Through information technology, real-time
     monitoring and coordination of the supply chain can be achieved to reduce operational risks and
     improve operational efficiency.

3.   Intensifying cooperation and common development: Actively developing long-term strategic
     partnerships with suppliers to establish mutual trust and win-win co-operation ecosystem. Provide
     support and training to help suppliers to improve their management level, technological innovation
     capability and operational efficiency, and promote the common growth of both parties.




                                                            Annual Report 2025 ZONQING Environmental Limited         77
     Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                Through the initiatives above, the Company will be able to effectively control supply chain risks, and improve
                supplier operational efficiency and product quality, so as to provide strong support for achieving sustainable
                development and maintaining competitive advantages.


          1.5 Information Security and Privacy Protection

                The Group has attached great importance to information security, customer privacy, trade secret management
                and data protection. The Group has continued to improve its information management system, which includes
                an information security awareness training system for employees: regular information security training is
                organised for employees to raise their awareness of information security.

                1.   Access control system: Strict access control policies are implemented to restrict access to sensitive
                     information.

                2.   Data classification and protection system: the Company’s data is classified and corresponding protection
                     measures are formulated. Emergency Response Plan: an emergency response plan is developed to deal
                     with information security incidents.

                The Group has formulated and implemented a Data Security and Confidentiality System, the Network and
                Information Security Management System, and the Network Security Emergency Response Plan. It has
                arranged for a designated information manager to be the main person responsible for the network and data
                security and confidentiality system, clearly delineated the level of information breach within the Group and
                graded the control, established comprehensive security measures for data use, storage, transmission and
                backup, as well as limited and controlled the security of staff system application through rules.

                The Company has taken corresponding measures to safeguard information security.

                1.   Network security protection: network security equipment, such as firewalls and intrusion detection
                     systems, are used to prevent network attacks and intrusions.

                2.   Data encryption: sensitive data is encrypted to ensure the security of data during transmission and
                     storage.

                3.   Employee background check: background checks are conducted on new employees to ensure employee
                     reliability.

                4.   Security audit: regular security audits are conducted to discover and repair information security
                     vulnerabilities.

                In addition, all office devices used by employees of Zonqing have been installed with Tianqing Firewall (天擎
                防火牆), and the information system applications are deployed separately from the data servers, where only
                the applications could be connected to external source while the data servers are not available to the public,
                thereby ensuring data security.

                In the future, the Group will continue to optimise its information security management system, enhance staff
                education on information security, invest in emerging technologies such as blockchain, artificial intelligence
                and IoT security, strengthen third-party partner management, and conduct regular information security
                assessment and vulnerability scanning to identify and address potential information security risks in a timely
                manner and to enhance and strengthen the Company’s information security protection capability.

1.6 Giving Back to the Community

    Since its establishment, the Group has been committed to public welfare cause. It has developed a sound
    public welfare engagement mechanism, and established a Public Welfare Committee comprising of key
    personnel from various departments, which is responsible for arranging and planning annual public welfare
    activities and ensuring that public welfare activities are carried out orderly. At the beginning of each year,
    the Public Welfare Committee, taking into account the Group’s business characteristics and social needs,
    determines the annual goals for public welfare, focusing on the areas of environmental protection and caring
    for the disadvantaged groups.

    In 2023, the Group donated RMB500,000 for the flood outbreak in Jilin Province, actively responding to
    government’s call to fulfil our corporate mission and social responsibility;

    In 2024, the Group actively participated in the prevention of urban flood and waterlogging, and stood on duty
    at sites to respond quickly, safeguarding people’s life;

    In 2025, adhering to the corporate social responsibility philosophy of “rooted in the local community and
    giving back to society”, the Group focused on the needs of frontline practitioners in the industry. Through
    a regular public welfare mechanism and project-based initiatives, the Group continued to fulfill its corporate
    social responsibilities, with an emphasis on areas such as migrant workers and community livelihoods,
    with an aim to build a “caring and down-to-earth” public welfare brand. The Group participated in urban
    environmental protection and cleaning activities to safeguard a green homeland through concrete actions.
    The Company’s high level of participation in public welfare activities and the positive social response they have
    garnered fully demonstrate our commitment to corporate social responsibility. In 2025, volunteers from the
    Group contributed approximately 16 hours of their time.

    In the future, the Company will continue to expand the scope of its public welfare activities, diversify
    the forms thereof, and deepen cooperation in key areas such as education assistance, elderly care and
    environmental protection, to develop distinctive brand philanthropic projects, and encourage more employees
    to engage in charitable causes, fulfilling our social responsibilities through practical actions, and spreading
    positive energy.


1.7 Operating with Integrity and Compliance

    Zonqing attaches importance to honesty and integrity and believes that integrity is an important guarantee
    for long-term development and winning the confidence of all parties. We advocate a corporate culture of
    honesty and integrity, with integrity being a corporate value embedded within the Company, and strictly abide
    by the Criminal Law of the People’s Republic of China and the Anti-Money Laundering Law of the People’s
    Republic of China and other anti-corruption and anti-money laundering regulations. The Party branch of the
    Company takes the lead and the legal affairs department carries out the supervision work. The Party branch
    of the Company has set up an anti-corruption reporting mailbox, which is regularly opened and checked
    by a dedicated person in charge to report the case, if any, to the Party branch, paying high attention to the
    construction of an anti-corruption and bribery system. We have gradually developed an anti-corruption risk
    assessment process that is suitable for the Group’s own development:

    ➢     The management of the Group provides adequate protection in terms of budget, staffing and
          preparation of working conditions.




                                                                Annual Report 2025 ZONQING Environmental Limited        79
     Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                ➢     The management of the Group advocates a corporate culture of honesty and integrity, specifying
                      the code of professional ethics for employees and stipulating the “six strict prohibitions” disciplinary
                      requirements, i.e., it is strictly prohibited from giving new year gifts (except greeting cards, emails and
                      text messages) to superiors, accepting banquets, gifts, gratuities and other properties from suppliers or
                      cooperative units in violation of the regulations, receiving and sending by logistics express, WeChat Red
                      Packets and other hidden means, using corporate funds to invite each other to banquets, organising or
                      participating in consumption and entertainment activities paid by corporate funds, and gambling.

                ➢     The Group conduct effective communication or training internally through a variety of forms (through
                      employee handbooks, publication of the Company’s rules and regulations, publicity or the internet,
                      etc.) to ensure that employees receive trainings on the topics of laws, regulations and codes of ethics so
                      that they understand the code of conduct; and to help employees identify legal and illegal, ethical and
                      non-ethical practices.

                ➢     In order to effectively prevent the occurrence of corruption practices, Zonqing and its subsidiaries
                      continuously improve its internal control system and conduct regular internal control self-assessment
                      work. When conducting functional management audits by each functional department of Zonqing,
                      it will identify key control points where corruption risks may occur, evaluate the construction and
                      performance of anti-corruption systems and processes, and promptly analyse and propose prevention
                      and control measures for major corruption incidents.

                In terms of anti-corruption, the Company has developed a detailed code of conduct, which clearly defines
                the boundaries of employees’ behaviour when engaging with customers, partners and others, and strictly
                prohibits any form of acceptance or solicitation of bribes. During the year, the Company organised a number
                of anti-corruption trainings and promotional activities, which enhanced employees’ awareness of integrity by
                explaining cases and interpreting regulations. In addition, a hotline and an online platform for whistle-blowing
                have also been set up to encourage employees and external persons to oversee and report corrupt practices,
                ensuring an honest and clean operating environment for the Company.

                Through such measures, the Company has achieved remarkable results in preventing bribery and corruption of
                suppliers, and maintained a good image and business order of the Company.

                In the future, in terms of anti-corruption, the Group will continue to focus on prevention, supplemented by
                combating corruption. It will continuously strengthen publicity and training on anti-corruption, repeatedly
                emphasize and publicize anti-corruption, and consistently instill the spirit of anti-corruption into employees, so
                that they have a fear of corruption. The Group will also enhance the supervision and punishment mechanisms
                against corruption, so that employees are unwilling, afraid and unable to be corrupt.

                The Group places great emphasis on the anti-bribery and anti-corrupt practices during the procurement
                process, and has established a sound prevention and control management system:

                1.    Control at source: Strictly implement supplier admission policies, conduct thorough reviews of
                      qualifications and credibility, and sign an integrity agreement with each supplier, specifying the
                      obligations to be incorrupt and the liabilities for breaching, to impose legal bindings on them.

                2.    Process monitoring: Set up a dedicated supervision team to conduct regular audits on contract
                      execution, payment and other processes, and to follow up and handle any anomalies as soon as they are
                      found. In addition, we promote open procurement process, and require suppliers to register a platform
                      account of the Group, so that all price enquiry documents are sent to designated mailboxes, ensuring
                      the process is transparent and compliant.

3.   Disciplinary action: Any person who is found to violate regulations, such as accept bribes, will be dealt
     with strictly in accordance with relevant regulations to strengthen internal disciplines.

4.   Collaborative anti-corruption: We actively promote the procurement system to suppliers, make public
     the telephone number for complaints and reports, and jointly support anti-corruption work. Through

In 2025, the Group continued to implement the Material Procurement Management System, and carried

measures include:

1.   Code of conduct: Eliminating non-compliant practices such as indiscriminate stockpiling of materials,
     unauthorised procurement, participation of family relatives in bidding, and unfair competition.

2.   Information accountability system: The procuring units shall be responsible for the reasonableness,
     rigour and confidentiality of the supporting information on the procurement. If errors in the information
     cause a loss, the Engineering Management Center will arrange an investigation and hold the responsible
     parties accountable.

3.   Ensuring no corruption: Making relevant reports on the procurement personnel who are found to accept
     bribes in accordance with the Integrity Agreement under the contract, providing regulatory protection
     for conducting business practises with integrity.

The Group has established a rigorous anti-corruption reporting and handling mechanism to ensure a smooth
reporting channel, and has implemented differentiated investigation and disciplinary procedures for managers
at different levels:

1.   Report Handling Process:

     For non-senior management: The Risk Management Center will report to the divisional leader within
     two working days upon receipt of the report under a real name and then commence investigation in
     accordance with the instructions and report the results of the investigation to superiors, who will then
     make a decision on the penalty.

     For senior management: For reports involving senior management, the Risk Management Center will
     report to the Audit Committee within two working days upon receipt of the report, and the Audit
     Committee will decide on further investigation. If necessary, a special investigation team may be set
     up or external professionals may be engaged to carry out the investigation, and finally the Audit
     Committee, together with the relevant departments, will make a decision on the penalty.

                2.   Privacy and Data Protection:

2.   TALENT TRAINING AND DEVELOPMENT

     2.1 Employment and Rights

        Zonqing has been upholding the people-oriented and endeavour-oriented concept and strictly abides by the
        Labour Law (《勞動法》), the Labour Contract Law (《勞動合同法》), and other relevant laws and regulations.
        It has formulated and executed a series of internal systems such as the Recruitment Management System, the
        Salary Management System, and the Personnel Management System, so that the management of employees
        is standardised and transparent.

        In terms of employment, adhering to the principle of equal pay for equal work and fair hiring, we strictly
        prohibit discrimination based on gender, nationality or other factors, and eliminate child labour and forced
        labour. During the recruitment process, we rigorously verify identities and resumes to ensure the accuracy of
        information. We also inform applicants of all job and compensation details to protect both parties’ right to
        know.

        The compensation system is transparent and well-structured, allowing employees to clearly understand their
        salary composition and salary accounting. The Company offers competitive compensation and benefits,
        as well as full pay for maternity leave and Chinese New Year holidays set by the Group in addition to the
        statutory ones and other benefits. We strictly implement the 8-hour workday system, manage overtime work
        in accordance with the law, and arrange compensatory leave or make-up leave.

        In terms of talent training, we have established a systematic training mechanism and enhanced employees’
        sense of belonging by organising diversified team building activities. In 2025, the Group did not have any
        irregularities in terms of remuneration packages, working hours and holidays, etc.. Moving forward, we will
        continue to improve the working environment and safeguard employees’ legal rights and interests.

2.2 Employee Care and Welfare

    In order to standardise and strengthen the welfare management of the Group’s employees, and give full
    play to the protective and motivational role of welfare, the Group has formulated a Welfare Management
    System, with employee benefits including statutory benefits and corporate benefits. The Group pays social
    insurance for all employees in accordance with the Labor Law of the People’s Republic of China and the Social
    Insurance Law of the People’s Republic of China and other relevant laws and regulations. Corporate benefits
    include medical check-up benefits, holiday benefits, wedding benefits and bereavement supports, birthday
    benefits, heating subsidies, communication subsidies, fuel subsidies, title subsidies, missed meal subsidies,
    accommodation subsidies, family visit subsidies, overseas subsidies, home rental subsidies, and summer heat
    protection subsidies, among others.


2.3 Employee Health and Safety

    Based on the principle of “safety first, prevention priority, comprehensive management and full participation”,
    Zonqing has built a scientific safety management system in accordance with the Safety Management System,
    GB/T 45001 and other standards, to implement the production safety responsibility system, so that no
    incidents of work-related injuries have occurred for three consecutive years.

    In terms of occupational health and safety, the Company has developed the Safety and Civilisation
    Construction Standards Manual and other guidelines. Each project is equipped with emergency response
    system, and employees are equipped with safety protection equipment such as safety helmets and insulated
    shoes. For the hazards such as dust, arc flash and noise, specific protective measures are adopted. Regular
    inspection and maintenance of fire protection and protective equipment are conducted to ensure they meet
    international or industry standards.

    For safety education, the Group organises trainings on health and hygiene and new safety laws for new hires
    annually to enhance the safety awareness of all staff. In addition, the Group provides medical insurance,
    social insurance and work injury insurance for employees in accordance with the laws, and purchases accident
    insurance for previously retired employees and interns, as well as arranges annual medical check-ups for all
    employees.

    The Group has recorded zero work-related fatalities over the past three years; during the Reporting Period,
    the lost days due to work injury was 116.5 days. In the future, the Company will increase capital investment
    in safety to promote digital safety control system, such as the MIB system for front-end risk prediction and
    cloud-based safety management system. It will improve the working conditions and reduce the labour
    intensity through new technologies and new techniques, so that the safety and health of employees can be
    effectively guaranteed.

         construction units and industry competent authorities.

         During the Year, we focused on the development of typical projects, including municipal infrastructure,
         housing for people’s livelihood, industrial supporting facilities, and energy-saving renovation projects. In
         the process of construction, we have fully implemented standardized management, and enforced Model
         Guidance, the “three-inspection system” and side-station supervision. By utilizing green construction
         techniques, such as BIM technology, water conservation and reuse, and dust and noise control, we have
         achieved zero safety incidents and 100% quality compliance. Despite of tight schedules, extensive overlapping
         work, demanding environmental requirements and other challenges, the project team completed key
         milestones on schedule through rational coordination and efficient execution, and multiple works were
         recognized as the “Safe and Civilized Standardized Construction Site” and the “High-Quality Structural
         Engineering Project”.

3.2 Addressing Climate Change

   Zonqing recognizes that climate change has become one of the important external factors affecting the
   long-term stable development of the industry, and the frequent occurrence of extreme weather, stricter
   environmental regulations, and accelerated green transition not only bring potential operational and
   compliance risks to the Group, but also give rise to new opportunities for low-carbon development and
   market innovation. Zonqing incorporates climate issues into its corporate sustainability strategy. Following
   frameworks such as the HKEX Implementation Guidance for Climate Disclosures, the Group actively builds
   a corporate climate response system, improves the top-down climate governance structure, identifies and
   manages climate-related risks and opportunities, and formulates practical climate strategies and sets metrics
   and targets to measure performance. Through systematic management, we continuously enhance the Group’s
   climate resilience, seize the opportunities of green transition, and achieve the sustainable development of
   corporate operations and environmental protection.




                                                             Annual Report 2025 ZONQING Environmental Limited      89
     Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                3.2.1 Governance

                     Zonqing understands that effective climate governance is the cornerstone of corporate sustainable
                     development, and has established a climate governance system led by the board of directors,
                     implemented by relevant working groups, and coordinated by relevant departments.

                     The board of directors of Zonqing is the highest decision-making body for climate governance,
                     overseeing the identification and assessment of climate-related risks and opportunities. The board of
                     directors reviews climate-related issues at least once a year and includes them in the annual ESG report
                     approval process. The board members have professional backgrounds in engineering management and
                     financial management, and continue to focus on the policy and market impact of climate change on the
                     construction and landscape industries.

                     The board of directors has established a relevant working group responsible for coordinating the
                     implementation of relevant departments, promoting the implementation of climate action within the
                     Group, and reporting to the board of directors on the progress of climate action.

                     In the future, the Group will consider further optimizing its governance structure, clearly defining the
                     roles and responsibilities of climate-related matters, strengthening internal management processes, and
                     enhancing the transparency and integrity of climate-related disclosures in line with evolving regulatory
                     requirements and best practices.

                3.2.2 Strategy

                     Zonqing mainly focuses on urban renewal services, urban operation and maintenance services, urban
                     planning and design, and cultural tourism business, and its business operations are closely related to
                     climatic conditions. In recent years, the frequent occurrence of extreme weather events and stricter
                     environmental protection policies have had a practical impact on the Group’s construction schedule,
                     costs and compliance operations. To this end, the Group incorporates climate factors into strategy
                     considerations, gradually established a climate risk identification and assessment mechanism, and seek
                     to understand the potential climate impact on its business under different climate paths through climate
                     scenario analysis.

                     ➢     Identification of climate-related risks and opportunities

                           Based on industry development trends and its own operating characteristics, Zonqing has
                           systematically identified and analyzed climate-related risks and opportunities, and gradually
                           incorporated relevant factors into the Group’s overall strategy planning system. Currently, the
                           Group has defined the time horizon of climate-related risk and opportunity assessment as
                           short-term (to 2030) and medium-term (to 2050), and is further studying on the long-term time
                           horizon. On this basis, the Group has conducted a comprehensive assessment of factors that may
                           affect the Group’s business operations and long-term development from multiple dimensions,
                           including policy and supervision, market changes, technological advancement and the physical
                           impact of climate change.




90   ZONQING Environmental Limited Annual Report 2025

       on Climate Change (IPCC) Sixth Assessment Report (AR6) and the International Energy Agency
       (IEA), the Group has selected the policy pathway from maintaining existing policies to net-zero
       transition, as well as the physical climate path from low to high emissions, to conduct climate
       scenario analysis to assess the resilience of its strategy and business model to climate change.

                     ➢     Scenario analysis and response strategy

                           Under different climate scenarios, the Group has assessed the likelihood and potential impact of
                           climate-related risks and opportunities, and has continuously improved corresponding response
                           strategies accordingly to enhance climate resilience and seize green transition opportunities. The
                           specific risks and opportunities identified and countermeasures are as follows:

                           Physical Risks


                           Physical Risks            Risk Category            Time Horizon             Potential Impact
                           Acute                     Extreme weather          Short-medium             Very low to low
                                                     (Typhoons,
                                                     windstorms, heavy
                                                     rains, blizzards)
                                                                     Risk Description
                           Extreme weather such as typhoons, windstorms, heavy rains, and blizzards affect construction
                           and operation and maintenance site operations, including construction progress, construction
                           difficulty, construction costs:

                           •      Outdoor operations are forced to stop work during extreme weather, causing delays and
                                  increased costs;

                           •      The construction sites are prone to water accumulation, and the foundation pit and road
                                  surfaces are uneven, and the slope is unstable, thus affecting the construction progress;

                           •      It will hinder the transportation of materials and machinery, and the slippery site will also
                                  increase the risk of safety accidents; earthworks and roadbed construction require rework
                                  after rainwater immersion;

                           •      During continuous heavy rains, the Group needs to invest more human and material
                                  resources to carry out drainage and prevention work, including quickly excavating the
                                  drainage system, mobilizing water pumps for pumping operations, thereby increasing the
                                  Group’s operating costs;

                           •      In the event of a blizzard, the Group’s sanitation business personnel need to carry out
                                  snow clearing operations in a timely manner to ensure smooth road traffic, thus increase
                                  operating expenses. Snow and low temperature environment at the construction site will
                                  cause the working surface to be slippery, making it difficult to start the machinery and
                                  reducing construction efficiency;

                           •      The occurrence of such extreme weather also hinders employee commuting and restricts
                                  on-site work arrangements, increasing safety management and operational risks.




92   ZONQING Environmental Limited Annual Report 2025
Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                                          Countermeasures
     •   Strengthen communication and cooperation with meteorological departments, establish
         an extreme weather warning mechanism, grasp meteorological changes in a timely
         manner, and provide a reference basis for on-site operation scheduling; issued early
         warning notices in advance to suspend outdoor high-risk operations;

     •   Actively promote the application of new construction technologies and equipment,
         improve the level of mechanized operations, reduce the dependence on manual
         operations in bad weather, and ensure construction continuity;

     •   Strengthen on-site fencing, drainage, slip prevention, and freeze protection inspections;
         and prepare emergency supplies such as raincoats, rain boots, snow melting agents, and
         warm materials; Reasonably adjust the construction plan to reduce the impact on the
         schedule, reduce property damage and safety risks, and ensure the safety of personnel
         and projects;

     •   Organize emergency drills every year to improve actual combat capabilities;

     •   During the project design and construction stage, we will focus on the application of
         green and low-carbon building concepts, and continue to optimize building energy
         conservation and resource utilization solutions;

     •   Increase the investment in the project on the early stage and carry out special
         reinforcement designs for temporary facilities, scaffolding and hoarding to improve the
         wind resistance and waterlogging resistance of the facilities.

     Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                           Physical Risks           Risk Category            Time Horizon             Potential Impact
                           Acute                    Extreme heat             Short-medium             Low to medium
                                                                   Risk Description
                           Overall, the frequent occurrence of extreme heat poses multiple challenges to construction
                           schedules, personnel safety, construction quality, and operating costs:

                           •     The frequent occurrence of extreme high temperature weather poses a direct threat to
                                 the health of outdoor workers, and the risk of heat stroke increases. The Group needs to
                                 strengthen heat prevention and cooling measures as well as medical support, which will
                                 increase the difficulty of on-site safety management and labour costs;

                           •     In a high-temperature environment, concrete pouring and curing operations are affected
                                 by rapid water evaporation, which is prone to quality problems such as dry shrinkage
                                 cracks and compressive strength. Accordingly, it is necessary to adjust the concrete mix
                                 design or adopt special curing measures, which in turn increases the risk of rework and
                                 associated costs;

                           •     The air conditioning load in office areas and temporary facilities continues to increase, and
                                 electricity consumption increases, pushing up operating costs;

                           •     The duration of high temperature weather is prolonged, and the long-term operation of
                                 on-site machinery and equipment can easily cause overheating failures, increasing the
                                 frequency of maintenance, thereby affecting the construction progress.
                                                                   Countermeasures
                           •     Strengthen communication with meteorological departments, issue high temperature
                                 warning and timely notify projects of off-peak construction;

                           •     Strengthen the heat stroke prevention measure and cooling materials, timely distribute
                                 cooling drinks, heat stroke prevention drugs and protective equipment, and establish a
                                 high-temperature daily health inspection mechanism;

                           •     Organize and carry out special training and emergency drills for heat stroke prevention
                                 and cooling to enhance the self-protection awareness of operators and the ability to
                                 provide first aid to heat stroke, and ensure the safety of personnel in hot weather.

     Physical Risks          Risk Category             Time Horizon             Potential Impact
     Acute                   Strong wind and           Short-medium             Very low to medium
                             sandstorm
                                             Risk description
     •    The spread of sand and dust has led to reduced visibility on site, reduced efficiency of
          material transportation and machinery operation, and hindered construction progress;

     •    The safety risks of high-altitude work are increased due to the impact of wind, and it is
          necessary to frequently suspend or adjust the operation plan, resulting in wasted working
          hours and delayed construction periods;

     •    The wear and tear of precision equipment due to windstorm weather and temporary
          facilities on site has intensified, and the equipment failure rate has increased, resulting in
          increased maintenance costs;

     •    Strong winds and dust weather are frequent, which has a direct impact on the
          construction site and green landscape.
                                            Countermeasures
     •    Strengthen communication with meteorological departments, obtain early warning
          information on strong winds and sandstorm in a timely manner, and adjust the operation
          plan in advance. Organize and carry out special inspections before the arrival of strong
          winds and dust weather, focusing on inspecting temporary hoarding and other facilities,
          and promptly discovering and eliminating various potential safety hazards;

     •    Protective coverage of on-site machinery and equipment to prevent sand and dust
          intrusion from causing failures and reducing wear;

     •    Carry out special safety training and emergency drills in windy and dusty weather,
          enhance the safety awareness of operators, and clarify the control requirements for
          suspending high-altitude work and lifting operations in bad weather.




                                                   Annual Report 2025 ZONQING Environmental Limited        95
     Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                           Physical Risks           Risk Category            Time Horizon             Potential Impact
                           Chronic                  Rising average           Short-medium             Low to medium
                                                    temperature
                                                                   Risk Description
                           The rising average temperature has put sustained pressure on the Group’s operating costs and
                           construction efficiency:

                           •     As the average temperature continues to rise, the energy consumption structure of
                                 construction sites and office areas changes. The increase in cooling demand in summer
                                 leads to an increase in air conditioning electricity consumption in temporary facilities,
                                 office areas, and workers’ living areas, resulting in a simultaneous increase in electricity
                                 bills and equipment maintenance costs. At the same time, although the demand for
                                 traditional heating decreases, some areas still need to heat themselves during low
                                 temperature periods, and related distributed heating equipment procurement, fuel
                                 consumption, and daily operation and maintenance expenses further push up operating
                                 costs;

                           •     The rising average temperature has an adverse effect on the operational stability of
                                 on-site machinery and equipment. Long-term high-temperature operation of equipment
                                 can easily cause overheating failures, increase maintenance frequency, and lead to
                                 construction efficiency being restricted;

                           •     Under high temperature and drought conditions, the difficulty of ecological restoration
                                 vegetation maintenance also increases. Newly planted seedlings need to increase watering
                                 frequency and take shading measures, irrigation water consumption will increase, and the
                                 cost of seedling maintenance will further increase. At the same time, projects such as river
                                 management and wetland restoration deteriorate due to the decline in water levels, and
                                 the water consumption and cost increase accordingly.
                                                                   Countermeasures
                           •     In the project design stage, prefabricated technology is actively used and quickly
                                 assembled on site, which not only improves construction efficiency, but also facilitates
                                 flexible adjustment of the layout according to high temperature weather changes in the
                                 later stage;

                           •     For office and living areas, the application of ground source heat pump systems is
                                 promoted, although the initial investment is high, but the long-term operating energy
                                 consumption is significantly lower than that of traditional air conditioners, which can
                                 continue to cope with the cooling demand brought about by high temperature weather;

                           •     Green construction design at project sites by integrating prefabrication and low-carbon
                                 technologies to enhance project adaptability to rising temperatures. At the same time,
                                 deepen collaboration with universities and research institutions to continuously optimize
                                 the application of relevant technologies, ensuring construction efficiency and worker
                                 comfort during high-temperature conditions.

Environmental, Social and Governance Report

     Physical Risks           Risk Category             Time Horizon             Potential Impact
     Chronic                  Sea level rise            Short-medium             Very low to medium
                                               Risk Description
     Sea level rise has a multifaceted impact on the Group’s coastal projects:

     •     At the project design and operation level, sea level rise puts forward higher requirements
           for drainage systems. The existing municipal pipe network and the internal drainage
           facilities of the project are facing the risk of backflow, and it is necessary to add
           anti-backflow facilities and upgrade the pumping station, and the drainage design
           standards will be improved accordingly, and the design and operation costs will increase;

     •     Rising sea levels have led to the risk of flooding of project assets and infrastructure in
           coastal areas, and stricter requirements have been put forward for foundation moisture
           protection and structural protection. Underground structures and low-lying areas need to
           be strengthened with waterproof treatment, and the durability of the project has been
           tested for a long time. At the same time, some assets may be scrapped early or relocated
           entirely due to frequent flooding, increasing maintenance and replacement costs.
                                               Countermeasures
     •     In the project design and construction stage, focus on optimizing drainage facility
           standards. For low-lying coastal areas, anti-backflow facilities and lifting pumping stations
           will be added to ensure the normal operation of the drainage system under extreme
           tide levels and heavy rainfall. In the early plan, the durability of the project was fully
           considered, and the underground structure, foundation and important infrastructure were
           designed to be moisture-proof and anti-corrosion to extend the service life of the asset;

     •     Strengthen communication and cooperation with meteorological and hydrological
           departments, dynamically monitor tide level changes, and provide data support for project
           operation and maintenance.

     •     Establish a carbon emission accounting methodology that covers Scope 1, 2 and main

                 3.2.3 Risk Management

                      The Group is gradually establishing a climate-related risk management mechanism. Currently, we have
                      initially formulated a climate risk identification list with reference to our business scope, geographical
                      distribution, and industry characteristics, and have tried to incorporate climate factors into our existing
                      risk management processes.

                      The Group will continue to optimize its climate risk management mechanism, improve its data collection
                      system and enhance its internal capabilities to continuously enhance the accuracy and soundness of
                      climate-related disclosures.


                       Risk Management             Measures
                       Process
                       Identify risks              The Group has initially identified climate-related physical risks and
                                                   transition risks through internal and external factors related to operations,
                                                   and has formed a structured risk list. This includes considerations of
                                                   business scope, geographic location, and industry context.
                       Risk assessment             At this stage, the Group mainly adopts a qualitative assessment method
                                                   to analyze the identified climate-related risks, focusing on their likelihood
                                                   of occurrence and potential impact on the Group’s operations, finances
                                                   and reputation. We have begun attempting climate scenario analysis to
                                                   assist in the identification and assessment of physical risks and transition
                                                   winds.
                       Risk Management and         The Working Group and relevant departments are responsible for
                       Response                    implementing mitigation measures and operational controls to
                                                   address the identified climate-related risks. These measures may
                                                   include operational adjustments, efficiency improvements, compliance
                                                   monitoring, and communication and coordination with suppliers or
                                                   stakeholders where appropriate. Climate-related factors are gradually
                                                   being integrated into operational planning and decision-making
                                                   processes.

                       governance integration      climate-related risks and mitigation progress as part of its ESG work.
                                                   This enables the Board to oversee the effectiveness of the Group’s
                                                   climate-related risk management approach and consider climate-related
                                                   matters in strategic decision-making.

3.3 Emissions Management

    The Group strictly adheres to the environmental protection policies, laws and regulations of the state and local
    governments, including but not limited to the Environmental Protection Law of the People’s Republic of China,
    the Law of People’s Republic of China on Prevention and Control of Water Pollution, the Law of People’s
    Republic of China on Prevention and Control of Atmospheric Pollution and the Law of People’s Republic of
    China on the Prevention and Control of Environmental Pollution Caused by Solid Waste, etc., and all indicators
    comply with the ISO14000 family of environmental management standards of the International Organisation
    for Standardisation. We take active measures to manage our emissions and fulfil our environmental
    responsibilities.

    The Group has established an environmental protection system parallel to our quality and safety assurance
    system, setting up an environmental protection leadership team headed by our managers, equipped with
    effective technical facilities, and has actively collaborated with local governments and environmental
    protection departments to fully control construction pollution, reduce sewage, air dust and noise pollution,
    and ensure compliance with national standards. Each project has dedicated persons responsible for the
    prevention of dust, noise and water pollution to ensure that appropriate measures are taken to reduce
    potential environmental impacts and to achieve environmental friendliness during the project.




                                                               Annual Report 2025 ZONQING Environmental Limited        107
      Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                 Management measures for environmental protection and green office:

                 1.   Construction site management: Strictly examine the pollution prevention work regarding dust, noise
                      and water pollution, ensuring that appropriate personnel in charge were assigned; reasonably arrange
                      cleaning vehicles on site and take covering measures during water sprinkling, loading and unloading of
                      fine particulate bulk materials for transportation and ensure that no spills are made along the way and
                      that construction site transport vehicles leave the site without mud and sand; use fences to separate
                      the construction area from non-construction areas to prevent affecting the environment outside the
                      construction area; ensure that site sewage is discharged after treatment and require secondary use of
                      construction water whenever possible.

                 2.   Resource intensive management: Optimize the process for selecting engineering materials and reduce
                      unnecessary travel by combing visits, limiting the number of participants and other means.

                 3.   Green and low-carbon office: Promote electronic office and set up used paper recycling bins to reduce
                      paper consumption.

                 4.   Standardized management of pollutants: Construction machinery uses clean energy to reduce exhaust
                      emissions; for domestic wastewater, we use flushing toilets and septic tanks for disinfection, and use
                      sewage pumping vehicles for transportation to the wastewater treatment plant for treatment.

                 The Group’s specific practices in waste separating, treatment and resource recovery:

                 1.   Types of waste: The waste generated from the business mainly includes non-hazardous greening waste,
                      i.e. tree trunks, branches and grass clippings from pruning, and construction waste.

                 2.   Resource recovery of greening waste: Implement classified utilization, namely, the small branches and
                      grass clippings are treated by direct burial and conversion into organic fertilizer; and larger branches are
                      sent to the biomass power plant for biomass power generation.

                 3.   Professional disposal of construction waste: Construction waste generated from on-site construction are
                      uniformly handed over to professional construction units for disposal.

                 4.   Reduction management throughout the process: Develop a construction site management plan to
                      classify and separate recyclable waste and use it for reprocessing or directly recycle it. In addition, we
                      set up recycling bins in the offices to promote full participation in waste minimisation. During the Year,
                      there was no violation of environment-related laws and regulations that had a significant impact on the
                      Group.




108   ZONQING Environmental Limited Annual Report 2025
  Environmental, Social and Governance Report

3.4 Use of Energy and Resources

    Zonqing attaches importance to energy conservation and emission reduction and implements the 3R principles
    of “Energy Consumption Reduction, Reuse and Recycling” in the course of its daily business. It regularly
    updates the Group’s policies and procedures, incorporating rules and guidelines for environmental protection
    into its daily workflow, creates a good green office culture and takes various measures to implement energy
    conservation:

    ➢    Post slogans to raise awareness of environmental protection. We post reminder slogans on company
         bulletin boards and in public areas, and place paper conservation slogans in restrooms to raise the
         environmental awareness of employees and visitors.

    ➢    Encourage green office and low-carbon travel. We encourage employees to adopt green office and
         green travel, turn off electronic devices when not in use or leaving the office to conserve energy, and
         advocate for carpooling during travel to reduce carbon emissions.

    ➢    Adopt energy-efficient equipment and pay attention to equipment maintenance. The procurement
         department gives priority to energy-efficient products, such as energy-saving motors, energy-saving
         lamps, energy-saving air conditioners, etc., when managing the purchase of electrical appliances on a
         daily basis, and adopts water-saving devices in office premises. Meanwhile, we regularly inspect electrical
         equipment to ensure operational efficiency.

    ➢    Reduce raw material consumption and improve reuse rates. The construction team uses materials
         available at the project site to build landscape facilities, such as using soil excavated at the construction
         site for the construction of rockeries.

    Management of energy resources consumption on site

    During the project construction process, an energy-saving and environmental protection construction plan
    is prepared before the project starts, which should be a separate chapter in the construction organisation
    design and approved in accordance with the regulations. The energy saving and environmental protection
    construction plan includes the followings:

    ➢    Environmental protection measures – Formulation of environmental management plans and emergency
         rescue plans, and adoption of effective measures to protect other facilities and resources such as cultural
         relics.

    ➢    Material saving measures – Develop material saving measures on the premise of ensuring the safety and
         quality of the project. These include optimising the construction programme, minimising construction
         waste and maximising the use of recyclable materials. The specific material saving measures are as
         follows:

         1.    To rationalise the procurement of materials, timing and batches of delivery to the site according to
               the progress of construction, stock availability, etc. to reduce stock.

         2.    Materials are stored in an orderly manner and in a suitable storage environment with appropriate
               measures.




                                                                Annual Report 2025 ZONQING Environmental Limited         109
      Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                      3.    Suitable means of transporting materials, proper loading and unloading methods, prevention of
                            damage and spillage, unloading near the site according to the layout of the site to avoid and
                            reduce secondary transportation.

                      4.    Adopting technical measures to increase the number of cycles of using materials.

                 ➢    Water conservation measures – Develop water conservation measures based on the water resource
                      status of the project site.

                 ➢    Energy saving measures – Construction energy saving planning, identification of targets and
                      development of energy saving measures. Specific energy saving measures during the construction
                      process are as follows:

                      1.    In the construction organisation design, the construction sequence and working surface should
                            be reasonably arranged so as to reduce the number of machines in the operation area and to
                            make full use of the common machine resources in the adjacent operation area. When arranging
                            construction techniques, priority should be given to those that consume electricity or other
                            construction techniques that consume less energy, so as to avoid the circumstances that the
                            rated power of the equipment is much higher than the used power or lead to overloading of the
                            equipment.

                      2.    Establish a management system for construction machinery and equipment, carry out electricity
                            and oil consumption metering, improve equipment filings and records, and conduct timely
                            maintenance work to keep machinery and equipment in a low-consumption and efficient
                            condition.

                      3.    It is advisable to use energy-saving materials for temporary facilities, and to use materials with
                            good thermal insulation for walls and roofs, so as to reduce the time and energy consumption of
                            air-conditioning and heating devices in summer and winter respectively.

                 ➢    Land conservation and construction land protection measures – Development of temporary land use
                      targets, construction overall layout planning and temporary land conservation measures, etc. Specific
                      land conservation and construction land protection measures are as follows:

                      1.    The overall layout of the construction is scientific and reasonable to reduce the area for the
                            temporary land use.

                      2.    The warehouses, processing areas, material yards, etc. at the construction site are located as
                            closely as possible to existing traffic routes to shorten transport distances.

                      3.    Temporary office and living quarters should be economical, aesthetically pleasing, occupy a
                            small area and have minimal impact on the surrounding landscape and environment. Living and
                            production areas should be separated with standard separation facilities.

                      4.    Use removable protective fencing at the construction site to provide temporary edge protection
                            and increase the material recycling rate.

                 We use clean energy sources such as natural gas, LPG, and electricity at the construction site whenever
                 possible, instead of coal and wood-fueled boilers.



110   ZONQING Environmental Limited Annual Report 2025
Environmental, Social and Governance Report

Water resource management

Water resource consumed for the maintenance of seedlings and office operations are the Group’s major water
consumption. All water resource consumed by the Group in production and office operations come from the
municipal pipeline network and do not involve water extraction. The following measures are in place for the
Group’s water resource management:

➢    In the course of daily office operations using water resource, in order to raise staff awareness of water
     conservation, the Group has installed “Water Conservation” signage in office areas to raise staff
     awareness of water conservation and actively used water saving devices to better conserve water.

➢    During the production process, the Group requires a large amount of water for the maintenance of
     seedlings. To increase the efficiency of ground watering, the Group has adopted micro-spray belts
     instead of traditional sprinklers, which reduces the amount of water used per square metre and
     effectively improves the quality of watering.

In the future, the Group will implement micro-sprinkler belts for watering ground and flowers in a large
number of projects where available so as to save water resource and reduce costs, while adopting a sponge
city concept to establish a rainwater collection system in the park for the maintenance of seedlings.

Construction material management

The raw materials used in the Group’s landscaping projects include seedlings, crushed stones for paving
foundations, concrete and stones for paving surfaces. The Group recycles construction materials, and the
construction team uses materials available at the project site to build landscape facilities and reduce the
consumption of raw materials, such as using soil excavated at the construction site for the construction of
rockeries or using the old foundation materials removed to replace the foundation materials used to build the
right-of-way. The details of the Group’s material use are as follows:

1.   The selection of suppliers and the procurement contracts should stipulate that air pollution-prone
     materials must be tested in accordance with national environmental standards, and only those materials
     that meet the standards can be accepted into the site to ensure that the impact of exhaust from
     materials on human health is minimized.

2.   Conduct overall layout planning for veneer-type materials before application so as to reduce the number
     of non-whole block materials.

3.   Coils, paints and all types of coatings must meet the requirements to avoid peeling and flaking. All types
     of paints and adhesives should be opened as they are used and closed in time when not in use.

4.   All types of preformed and embedded processes should be performed in line with the construction of
     the structure as far as possible.




                                                          Annual Report 2025 ZONQING Environmental Limited        111
      Environmental, Social and Governance Report

                 5.   Wooden products and materials for wooden decoration, glass and other structures should be sourced
                      from factories or custom-made.

                 6.   Promote the use of ready-mixed concrete and commercial mortar.

                 7.   Roofing materials and external wall materials have good waterproofing and thermal insulation
                      properties.

                 8.   Strengthen the nodes of the thermal insulation system and the enclosure structure to minimise the
                      thermal bridging effect. Different thermal insulation materials and systems are used for different parts of
                      the building in order to achieve an economical solution.

                 9.   After use, paints, adhesives, water-based primers, thinners and solvents should be closed and stored in a
                      timely manner, and waste materials should be removed in a timely manner.

                 In the future, the Group will continue to monitor energy consumption, effectively control the use of resources
                 and energy, and continuously promote energy conservation and emission reduction to achieve sustainable
                 development.

      INDEPENDENT AUDITOR’S REPORT
      TO THE SHAREHOLDERS OF
      ZONQING ENVIRONMENTAL LIMITED
      (Incorporated in the Cayman Islands with limited liability)


      OPINION

      We have audited the consolidated financial statements of ZONQING Environmental Limited (the “Company”) and its
      subsidiaries (the “Group”) set out on pages 135 to 218, which comprise the consolidated statement of financial position
      as at 31 December 2025, the consolidated statement of profit or loss, the consolidated statement of profit or loss and
      other comprehensive income, the consolidated statement of changes in equity and the consolidated cash flow statement
      for the year then ended and notes, comprising material accounting policy information and other explanatory information.

      In our opinion, the consolidated financial statements give a true and fair view of the consolidated financial position of
      the Group as at 31 December 2025 and of its consolidated financial performance and its consolidated cash flows for the
      year then ended in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards as issued by the International Accounting Standards Board
      (“IASB”) and have been properly prepared in compliance with the disclosure requirements of the Hong Kong Companies
      Ordinance.


      BASIS FOR OPINION

      We conducted our audit in accordance with Hong Kong Standards on Auditing (“HKSAs”) as issued by the Hong
      Kong Institute of Certified Public Accountants (“HKICPA”). Our responsibilities under those standards are further
      described in the Auditor’s responsibilities for the audit of the consolidated financial statements section of our report.
      We are independent of the Group in accordance with the HKICPA’s Code of Ethics for Professional Accountants (the
      “Code”), as applicable to audits of financial statements of public interest entities. We have also fulfilled our other ethical
      responsibilities in accordance with the Code. We believe that the audit evidence we have obtained is sufficient and
      appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion.


      KEY AUDIT MATTERS

      Key audit matters are those matters that, in our professional judgement, were of most significance in our audit of the
      consolidated financial statements of the current period. These matters were addressed in the context of our audit of the
      consolidated financial statements as a whole, and in forming our opinion thereon, and we do not provide a separate
      opinion on these matters.

      INFORMATION OTHER THAN THE CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL STATEMENTS AND
      AUDITOR’S REPORT THEREON

      The directors are responsible for the other information. The other information comprises all the information included in
      the annual report, other than the consolidated financial statements and our auditor’s report thereon. We obtained all
      of the other information prior to the date of this auditor’s report apart from a full set of finalised annual report not yet
      received. This remaining information is expected to be made available to us after that date.

      Our opinion on the consolidated financial statements does not cover the other information and we do not express any
      form of assurance conclusion thereon as part of our engagement to audit the consolidated financial statements. We
      have performed an assurance engagement on the disclosed continuing connected transactions that form part of the
      other information and provided a separate assurance practitioner’s conclusion thereon that is included within the other
      information.

      In connection with our audit of the consolidated financial statements, our responsibility is to read the other information
      identified above and, in doing so, consider whether the other information is materially inconsistent with the consolidated
      financial statements or our knowledge obtained in the audit or otherwise appears to be materially misstated.

      If, based on the work we have performed on the other information that we obtained prior to the date of this auditor’s
      report, we conclude that there is a material misstatement of this other information, we are required to report that fact.
      We have nothing to report in this regard.


      RESPONSIBILITIES OF THE DIRECTORS FOR THE CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL
      STATEMENTS

      The directors are responsible for the preparation of the consolidated financial statements that give a true and fair view
      in accordance with IFRS Accounting Standards as issued by the IASB and the disclosure requirements of the Hong Kong
      Companies Ordinance and for such internal control as the directors determine is necessary to enable the preparation of
      consolidated financial statements that are free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error.

      In preparing the consolidated financial statements, the directors are responsible for assessing the Group’s ability to
      continue as a going concern, disclosing, as applicable, matters related to going concern and using the going concern
      basis of accounting unless the directors either intend to liquidate the Group or to cease operations, or have no realistic
      alternative but to do so.

      The directors are assisted by the Audit Committee in discharging their responsibilities for overseeing the Group’s financial
      reporting process.

AUDITOR’S RESPONSIBILITIES FOR THE AUDIT OF THE CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL
STATEMENTS

Our objectives are to obtain reasonable assurance about whether the consolidated financial statements as a whole are
free from material misstatement, whether due to fraud or error, and to issue an auditor’s report that includes our opinion.
This report is made solely to you, as a body, and for no other purpose. We do not assume responsibility towards or accept
liability to any other person for the contents of this report.

Reasonable assurance is a high level of assurance, but is not a guarantee that an audit conducted in accordance with
HKSAs will always detect a material misstatement when it exists. Misstatements can arise from fraud or error and are
considered material if, individually or in the aggregate, they could reasonably be expected to influence the economic
decisions of users taken on the basis of these consolidated financial statements.

As part of an audit in accordance with HKSAs, we exercise professional judgement and maintain professional scepticism
throughout the audit. We also:

–    Identify and assess the risks of material misstatement of the consolidated financial statements, whether due to fraud
     or error, design and perform audit procedures responsive to those risks, and obtain audit evidence that is sufficient
     and appropriate to provide a basis for our opinion. The risk of not detecting a material misstatement resulting from
     fraud is higher than for one resulting from error, as fraud may involve collusion, forgery, intentional omissions,
     misrepresentations, or the override of internal control.

–    Obtain an understanding of internal control relevant to the audit in order to design audit procedures that are
     appropriate in the circumstances, but not for the purpose of expressing an opinion on the effectiveness of the
     Group’s internal control.

–    Evaluate the appropriateness of accounting policies used and the reasonableness of accounting estimates and
     related disclosures made by the directors.

–    Conclude on the appropriateness of the directors’ use of the going concern basis of accounting and, based on
     the audit evidence obtained, whether a material uncertainty exists related to events or conditions that may cast
     significant doubt on the Group’s ability to continue as a going concern. If we conclude that a material uncertainty
     exists, we are required to draw attention in our auditor’s report to the related disclosures in the consolidated
     financial statements or, if such disclosures are inadequate, to modify our opinion. Our conclusions are based on the
     audit evidence obtained up to the date of our auditor’s report. However, future events or conditions may cause the
     Group to cease to continue as a going concern.

–    Evaluate the overall presentation, structure and content of the consolidated financial statements, including the
     disclosures, and whether the consolidated financial statements represent the underlying transactions and events in a
     manner that achieves fair presentation.

–    Plan and perform the group audit to obtain sufficient appropriate audit evidence regarding the financial information
     of the entities or business units within the Group as a basis for forming an opinion on the consolidated financial
     statements. We are responsible for the direction, supervision and review of the audit work performed for purposes
     of the group audit. We remain solely responsible for our audit opinion.

      describe these matters in our auditor’s report unless law or regulation precludes public disclosure about the matter or
      when, in extremely rare circumstances, we determine that a matter should not be communicated in our report because
      the adverse consequences of doing so would reasonably be expected to outweigh the public interest benefits of such
